### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 9 日

1 群益投顧 
ASML (ASML US) 與台積電 (2330 TT) 共同推動 High NA EUV 將自現行的 6 吋光罩，
轉移至更大的 12 吋 光 罩。 目標於 2031 年之前建立 12 吋 光 罩 試 產 線(pilot line) ， 並 於
2033 年之前實現微影系統全面就緒。台積電並表示自 2030 年 起 ， 將 在 先 進 製 程 量 產
中導入 ASML 的 High NA  EUV 。台積電與 ASML 共同發起 6 吋光罩轉移至 12 吋光
罩 的 規 格 轉 移 ， 其 戰 略 意 義 在 於 ， 製 訂 規 格 和 標 準 ， 確 保 台 積 電 技 術 領 先 地 位 。 因 此
對 台 積 電 的 投 資 評 等 Buy ，目標價 3,200 元。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

ASML 與 台 積 電 共 同 推 動 High NA EUV 將 自 現 行 的 6 吋 光 罩 ， 轉 移 至 更 大
的 12 吋 光 罩 ：  
09/07/2026 ASML 與 台 積 電 於 BACUS 國 際 光 學 工 程 學 會(SPIE) 先 進 微 影 技
術 研 討 會 前 宣 布 發 起 產 業 合 作 ， 共 同 推 動High NA EUV 將 自 現 行 的 6 吋光
罩 ， 轉 移 至 更 大 的 12 吋 光 罩 ， 預 期 將 進 一 步 提 升 晶 圓 廠 的 生 產 力 、 降 低 晶
片 製 造 成 本 ， 並 消 除 需 要 2 張 6 吋 光 罩 在 High NA EUV 曝 光 時 進 行 拼 接 的
限 制，使 整 個 半 導 體 產 業 受 惠。此 轉 移 能 讓 先 進 晶 片 製 造 服 務 提 供 者 充 分 利
用 High NA EUV 的 優 勢 ， 更 提 升 未 來 世 代 先 進 晶 片 的 成 本 效 益 。 
目標於 2031 年 之 前 建 立 12 吋 光 罩 試 產 線(pilot line) ，並於 2033 年 之 前 實
現 微 影 系 統 全 面 就 緒 ， 以 支 援 先 進 製 程 量 產 。 台 積 電 並 表 示 自2030 年 起 ，
將 在 先 進 製 程 量 產 中 導 入 ASML 的 High NA 技 術 。  
12 吋光罩 使 High NA 效 率 提 升 ：  
現行 EUV 設備的 NA(Numerical Aperture ， NA) 為 0.33 ， High NA 則 提 高 到
0.55 。 NA 數 值 越 高 ， 解 析 度 也 越 高 、 曝 光 時 能 呈 現 的 細 節 愈 多 。ASML 資
料顯示 High NA 可 將 最 小 解 析 尺 寸 從 現 行 EUV 的約 13 奈 米 進 一 步 縮 小 到
8 奈 米 ， 也 就 是 能 在 晶 圓 上 印 出 更 細 微 的 電 路 圖 形 。 部 分 原 本 需 要2 次 甚 至
更多次曝光才能完成的圖形，也有機會改以單次曝光處理，降低製程複雜
度 、 缺 陷 風 險 與 生 產 周 期 。 
舉 例 來 說 ，0.33 NA EUV 機 台 ， 至 少 需 要 4 張 光 罩 、 分 多 次 曝 光 才 能 完 成
A14 、 A10 金 屬 層 圖 形 ；High NA EUV ， 只 要 1 張 光 罩 就 能 完 成 相 同 圖 形 。
但 解 析 度 提 高 ，High NA 的 光 學 設 計 讓 單 次 可 曝 光 範 圍 縮 小 到 原 本EUV 的
50% ， 製 作 大 顆 晶 片 時 可 能 必 須 分 成2 次 曝 光 ， 再 把 圖 形 拼 接 起 來 。 這 也 是
台積電與 ASML 此 次 推 動 12 吋 光 罩 的 原 因 ， 相 較 現 行 6 吋 光 罩 ， 放 大 光 罩
尺 寸 預 期 能 進 一 步 提 升 晶 圓 廠 生 產 力、降 低 晶 片 製 造 成 本，並 消 除 High NA
曝 光 時 的 拼 接 限 制 。 
台 積 電 預 計 2030 年 開 始 使 用 High NA  EUV ， 推估 High NA 將 會 在 A10 或
A10 以 下 的 先 進 製 程 採 用：  
台積電預計 2030 年 開 始 導入 High NA  EUV ，比 12 吋 光 罩 微 影 系 統 預 計 全
面就緒的 2033 年 更 早 。 這 代 表 台 積 電 初 期 導 入High NA 時 ， 仍 會 先 使 用 現
行 6 吋 光 罩 ， 之 後 再 隨 12 吋 光 罩 與 相 關 系 統 成 熟 逐 步 轉 換 。

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 9 日

2 群益投顧 
根 據 台 積 電 目 前 公 開 的 製 程 藍 圖 ，A13 與 A12 製 程 都 預 計 2029 年 量 產 。 其
中 A13 是 A14 平 台 的 延 伸 ，A12 則 將 超 級 電 軌(Super Power Rail) 背面供電
技術導入 A14 平 台 。 因 此 N2 、 A16 、 A14 、 A13 與 A12 都 早 於 台 積 電 預 計
2030 年 導 入 High NA ， 推 估 High NA 將 會 在 A10 或 A10 以 下 的 先 進 製 程 採
用。  
Intel (INTC US) 和 Samsung (005930 KS) 領 先 台 積 電 採 用 High NA EUV ，但
也 同 時 參 與 12 吋 光 罩 開 發 ：  
Intel 在 2024 年 宣 布 完 成 全 球 首 套 商 用 High NA EUV 設 備 的 組 裝 ；2026 年
High NA 已 經 正 式 進 入 量 產 ， 並 用 於 部 分 18A 製 程 層 。 是 目 前 High NA 導
入 進 度 最 快 的 晶 片 製 造 商 。Samsung 則預計 2028 年導入 DRAM 量 產 ； 台 積
電選擇在 2030 年 導 入 。Intel 和 Samsung 也 同 時 表 示 與 ASML 及 供 應 鏈 合
作 ， 推 動 未 來 轉 向 大 尺 寸 光 罩 。 
台 積 電 與 ASML 共 同 發 起 6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋 光 罩 的 規 格 轉 移，將 領 導 業
界 規 格 ：  
6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋光罩 的 這 項 升 級 非 單 純 的 尺 寸 放 大 ， 而 是 面 臨 生 態 系
與 物 理 極 限 的 多 重 挑 戰 。 整 體 供 應 鏈 重 構 ， 包 括 基 板 與 材 料 以 及 設 備 等 等 。
12 吋 光 罩 石 英 基 板 的 平 坦 度 、 缺 陷 密 度 與 熱 膨 脹 係 數 控 制 難 度 倍 增 。 製 程
設 備 如 薄 膜 沉 積、蝕 刻、清 洗 與 自 動 化 搬 運 設 備 皆 需 重 新 開 發。後 續 檢 測 與
量測極限檢測難度也增加，更大面積的圖形缺陷檢測（ Inspection ）與修補
技 術 需 要 突 破 。 在 12 吋 面 積 上 維 持 奈 米 級 的 對 位 精 度 與 線 寬 均 勻 性 極 為 困
難   
電 子 設 計 自 動 化 (EDA) 工 具 必 須 重 新 適 應 大 尺 寸 曝 光 視 場 與 資 料 處 理 。 12 
吋 光 罩 的 圖 形 檔 案 與 運 算 複 雜 度 將 大 幅 上 升 。 
台積電與 ASML 共 同 發 起 6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋 光 罩 的 規 格 轉 移，其 戰略意
義在於 ，台 積 電 為 全 球 最 大 晶 圓 代 工 廠，為 先 進 製 程 最 大 的 供 應 商，可 以 領
導 光 罩 基 底 供 應 商 、 光 罩 檢 測 設 備 商， 甚 至 EDA 廠 等 整 個 生 態 系 ，除 共同
分攤 ， 跨 越 升 級 12 吋 光 罩 的 龐 大 研 發 成 本外 ， 製 訂 規 格 和 標 準 ， 確 保 台 積
電 技 術 領 先 地 位 。因 此 對 台 積 電 的投 資 評 等 Buy ， 目 標 價 3,200 元。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Summary: NYPCB released its August results (for pre-tax profit and net profit) after 
market hours on 2 October mainly due to recent volatile share price movements. Net profit 
in August 2026 came in at TWD1,092m (EPS: TWD1.69), up 665% YoY, with pre-tax profit 
at TWD1,486m (pre-tax margin at 27.8%). The August pre-tax margin of 27.8% is close to 
our current forecast (28.5%/32.9% in 3Q26E/4Q26E). As we expect limited impact from 
non-ops and on opex in August, we infer the gross margin in August should have reached 
to c.30-31% (vs. our forecast c.30.3/34.4% in 3Q26E/4Q26E). This echoes our previous 
notes (Spec upgrade to drive additional ABF substrate demand); we expect NYPCB’s 
gross margin will recover to c.30% after Kunshan’s yield rate improvement in August. As 
such, we believe the August results to be largely on track with market expectations. 
However, we believe the share price upside will come from BT substrate, where we should 
see better progress in its implementing price hikes now (vs. limited visibility earlier in 
September). We expect NYPCB to raise ASP of BT substrates by single digits QoQ in 
4Q26 and +20% QoQ in 1Q27. This is aligned with our previous comment (Clarification on 
“no intention to hike price from 4Q26”) that we expect BT substrate prices will accelerate 
again in 2027 despite a lower-than-expected price hike in 4Q26E. We view NYPCB’s 
August results announcement as a near-term price catalyst. We have a Buy rating (1) on 
NYPCB with a 12-month TP of TWD2,460. Downside risk: weaker-than-expected demand 
for switches/PCs/ smartphones.

M
Update
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales  +22% MoM/
+206% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chroma's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September revenue was NT$6,860mn (22% MoM/+206% YoY), a record
high.
• 3Q26 revenue came in at  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY),
significantly higher than  our estimate at NT$14,772mn (+9% QoQ/+130%
YoY) and consensus at NT$14,555mn (+8% QoQ/+127% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe that the strength in 3Q26 was mainly driven by the
semiconductor and photonics business, especially SL T, metrology,
transceiver/CPO testing, etc.
• We think that Chroma might start to ship FT handlers and ovens in small
volume from late 2026/early 2027, which represent new business
opportunities for the coming years.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

기업분석 2025.09.28 www.daishin.com

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 3
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

Bloom Energy overview

As of year-end 2025, Bloom's product backlog grew about 140% YoY to roughly
US$6bn. Amid the surging demand, Bloom is improving backlog conversion and
stepping up capacity expansion to capture the strong growth opportunity. In 2Q26,
Bloom raised its full-year revenue guidance to US$3.9-4.2bn, up from US$3.4-3.8bn,
and plans to double annual capacity from about 1GW in 2025, split across its California

and Delaware sites, to 2GW by the end of 2026; and will further expand the capacity
to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our channel checks.

it leased a 158,000-square-foot facility at Encyclopedia Circle to expand its
manufacturing operations. The size of the new facility is notable since it is nearly

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

its new product offerings, higher content per rack of high-speed products, and
strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.

Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác 
tăng bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế 
gia tăng được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 
182 điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp từ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất và 6 điểm mới tại 
T2 Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Quốc và Gia Bình tiếp tục mở 
ra dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh được kỳ vọng 
tác động tích cực lên biên lợi nhuận của AST. Chúng tôi dự phóng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp đạt 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 
48%/17% yoy.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với AST, giá mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco là một 
trong những doanh nghiệp có quy mô mạng 
lưới điểm kinh doanh lớn trong lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ phi hàng không tại Việt Nam, với 141 
điểm kinh doanh tại 6 sân bay quốc tế tính 
đến cuối tháng 6/2026. Hoạt động cốt lõi của 
công ty bao gồm bán lẻ bách hóa lưu niệm 
(BHLN), hàng miễn thuế, dịch vụ ăn uống và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Ngoài ra, AST còn 
tham gia lĩnh vực suất ăn hàng không thông 
qua khoản đầu tư tại công ty liên kết VinaCS. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Doanh thu trên các điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu tăng nhờ lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa dự phóng tăng 
11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng trưởng mới, với số điểm kinh doanh AST 2026 dự kiến đạt 145 
điểm và tăng lên 182 điểm vào 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Biên lợi nhuận gộp cải thiện lên 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027 nhờ mở rộng hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ và tối ưu vận hành. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

Biểu đồ 5. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh của AST

Nguồn:AST

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng tăng trưởng lưu lượng hành khách, đặc biệt là sự gia tăng tỷ 
trọng khách quốc tế, sẽ thúc đẩy doanh thu và hiệu quả kinh doanh của AST 
trong giai đoạn 2026–2030. Theo Cục Hàng không Việt Nam, lượng khách thông 
qua các cảng hàng không trong 7T2026 đạt hơn 76 triệu lượt (+7% yoy), trong 
đó khách quốc tế đạt gần 30 triệu lượt (+11% yoy), trong khi khách nội địa ghi 
nhận tăng trưởng 4% yoy. Xu hướng này được hỗ trợ bởi nhu cầu du lịch quốc 
tế, chính sách thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng bay, trong khi tăng 
trưởng kinh tế và thu nhập tiếp tục tạo nền tảng cho nhu cầu đi lại trong nước. 
Trên cơ sở đó, chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế và nội địa tại các thị 
trường AST khai thác tăng bình quân lần lượt 11% và 5%/năm đến 2030, qua đó 
nâng dần tỷ trọng khách quốc tế trong tổng lưu lượng, thúc đẩy doanh 
thu/điểm kinh doanh nhờ: (1) lưu lượng hành khách qua cảng hàng không gia 
tăng và (2) tỷ trọng khách quốc tế cao hơn hỗ trợ doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

 Biểu đồ 7. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh AST (tỷ VND)

Nguồn: AST  Nguồn: AST

Mở rộng mạng lưới tại các nhà ga 
hiện hữu và sân bay mới, gia tăng 
quy mô kinh doanh đến 2030 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng chu kỳ mở rộng hạ tầng hàng không giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ 
tạo dư địa gia tăng quy mô kinh doanh cho AST, với số điểm kinh doanh dự 
phóng đạt 145 điểm vào cuối 2026 và tăng lên 182 điểm vào năm 2030. Cụ thể: 
- Trong ngắn hạn, tăng trưởng năm 2026 được hỗ trợ bởi việc ghi nhận 
đủ năm doanh thu từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
trong năm 2025, cùng 6 điểm mới tại T2 Nội Bài trong năm 2026, trong 
đó có 2 cửa hàng miễn thuế và 2 cửa hàng Memories of Vietnam khai 
trương trong 2Q2026. 
- Trong trung và dài hạn, các dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành, Cảng HKQT 
Phú Quốc nâng cấp mở rộng và Cảng HKQT Gia Bình sẽ góp phần thúc 
đẩy ngành hàng không Việt Nam nói chung, bổ sung mặt bằng thương 
mại, mở ra cơ hội phát triển các mảng kinh doanh cốt lõi cho AST nói 
riêng bao gồm các mảng bán lẻ, miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Với kinh nghiệm khai thác 141 điểm kinh doanh 
tại 6 sân bay quốc tế và lịch sử trúng thầu tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất, T2 Nội 
Bài trong thời gian qua, chúng tôi kỳ vọng AST có khả năng cạnh tranh 
cao trong việc đấu thầu quyền khai thác mặt bằng tại các sân bay mới, 
đặc biệt là Gia Bình, qua đó duy trì vị thế và thị phần kinh doanh phi 
hàng không tại khu vực phía Bắc khi một phần lưu lượng hành khách 
được phân bổ lại từ Nội Bài.

Nguồn: KBSV tổng hợp  Nguồn: AST , KBSV dự phóng

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ mở rộng biên lợi nhuận  
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng biên lợi nhuận của AST tiếp tục cải thiện trong 2026 - 2027, 
nhờ định hướng mở rộng các mảng có biên lợi nhuận tốt như hàng miễn thuế 
và phòng chờ thương gia, kết hợp tối ưu hiệu quả vận hành tại các điểm kinh 
doanh. Cụ thể: (1) AST ưu tiên gia tăng số điểm kinh doanh hàng miễn thuế 
nhằm đón đầu tăng trưởng nhanh và ổn định của lượng khách quốc tế, qua đó 
nâng đóng góp lợi nhuận từ hoạt động mua sắm tại sân bay, (2) mở rộng mạng 
lưới phòng chờ, đồng thời gia tăng lượng khách sử dụng trên nền năng lực phục 
vụ hiện hữu và (3) tối ưu hóa hiệu quả hoạt động thông qua cải thiện bố trí mặt 
bằng, danh mục sản phẩm và kiểm soát chi phí tại các điểm kinh doanh, qua 
đó tăng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thu được trên mỗi khách hàng.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá 
mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu AST, với giá mục tiêu 76,500 
VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 
21/09/2026, dựa trên triển vọng tăng trưởng từ lượng khách quốc tế, mở rộng 
mạng lưới điểm kinh doanh và khả năng cải thiện biên lợi nhuận của doanh 
nghiệp. Chúng tôi sử dụng phương pháp định giá từng phần (SOTP), bao gồm 
DCF cho hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi của AST và P/E mục tiêu 11x đối với phần 
lợi nhuận thuộc sở hữu AST tại VinaCS. Các giả định chính gồm: (1) lượng khách 
quốc tế/nội địa tăng trung bình 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030, (2) số điểm kinh 
doanh tăng từ 145 điểm cuối 2026 lên 182 điểm năm 2030, với giả định AST 
trúng thầu mặt bằng tại cả Long Thành và Gia Bình và (3) biên lợi nhuận gộp 
2026/2027 đạt 64.7/65.5%. Đối với VinaCS, chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhà máy suất ăn 
Long Thành sẽ hỗ trợ tăng trưởng lợi nhuận trong trung và dài hạn khi dự kiến 
có thể bắt đầu đóng góp lợi nhuận cho AST từ 2028.

Rủi ro đối với định giá bao gồm: (1) AST không giành được quyền khai thác tại 
Long Thành và Gia Bình như kỳ vọng, (2) lưu lượng khách dịch chuyển giữa các 
sân bay mới và hiện hữu khiến doanh thu tại một số điểm kinh doanh bị sụt 
giảm, (3) cạnh tranh gia tăng từ các doanh nghiệp kinh doanh dịch vụ phi hàng 
không khác và (4) căng thẳng địa chính trị kéo dài, giá dầu tăng gây áp lực lên 
hoạt động hàng không và chi tiêu của hành khách.

Document 254: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

USD/JPY: Yen re-rating enters a pause
Spot One month bias 1M 3M 6M 1
USD/JPY 158.46 Neutral 158.00 158.00 158.00 15
September proved a wild month for USD/JPY, where runaway yen
optimism brieﬂy saw 153, before a hawkish Fed and a dovish Bank
of Japan hike sent it back to 158. What we’ve learned this year is
that Japan and the US are uncomfortable with the pair above 160.
But a move to 150 requires less hawkishness from the Fed and a

much higher policy rate in Japan. That may not emerge until
2Q27.
Domestically, growth is picking up in Japan, but we doubt the BoJ is
ready to accelerate its tightening cycle. We think the next hike (to
1.50%) comes in January, or slightly earlier on 18 December.
30 October is a big date for USD/JPY. No BoJ hike and no news of
GPIF fund re-allocation to Japan could weigh heavily on the yen.

With energy prices staying higher for longer, it increasingly looks
like the Bank of England might hike at its 5 November meeting. If it
does, we suspect another hike will be coming in February – taking
the policy rate to 4.25%. Central to the BoE is inﬂation approaching
4%, a level where BoE research has found that second-round

inﬂation eﬀects become greater.
The Bank delivering on expectations of a rate hike should be a
sterling positive – cementing its status as a G10 high-yielder.
28 October is a UK event risk in the form of Prime Minister Andy
Burnham’s ﬁrst budget. Cost of living support measures should be
announced, but we expect them to be funded, avoiding a Gilt
market sell-oﬀ.

Independent euro weakness coming through on the back of the
French debt crisis is helping to solidify this correction in EUR/JPY.
Having rallied over 60% in six years, EUR/JPY should now enter a
more consolidative environment – perhaps in a 175-182 range.
From the euro side, downside risks exist from the European Central

Bank's reaction function. Should the ECB feel the need to curtail its
tightening cycle (we look for one last hike to 2.75% in December),
slow its quantitative tightening scheme or even buy bonds again,
EUR/JPY could see a sizeable break lower.
In Japan, any faster Bank of Japan tightening or the GPIF
announcing an increase to its 50% weighting for domestic assets
would help the yen.

emergency measures, EUR/GBP could trade all the way to 0.82.

more hawkish BoE than initially expected should keep EUR/GBP in a

headlines that are typically welcomed by sterling.

EUR/CHF: French debt crisis sees CHF win new
friends
Spot One month bias 1M 3M 6M 12M
EUR/CHF 0.9318 Neutral 0.93 0.93 0.94 0.95
It looks like EUR/CHF has put in an important top at 0.95 in
September. Recall that franc weakness was being driven by ever
higher global interest rates (Swiss rate hikes would lag) and also
low volatility favouring franc-funded carry trades. The French debt
crisis has been enough to reverse both those factors. On a view

that the French risk is unresolved this year, expect EUR/CHF to
bounce around in a 0.92-0.94 range.
The Swiss National Bank is showing no signs of turning more
hawkish. CPI is forecast at 0.8% in both 2027 and 2028.
Were French risk to become extreme, expect the SNB to buy
EUR/CHF again in the 0.90/91 region – though in small amounts.

EUR/SEK: Relief rally in cheap SEK
Spot One month bias 1M 3M 6M 12
EUR/SEK 11.20 Mildly Bearish 11.15 11.15 11.00 10
EUR/SEK has retreated from its highs as French risk hit the euro.
While the krona remains a less attractive expression of eurozone
ﬁscal risk than the CHF or GBP, it was also notably cheap versus
short-term fair value at the end of last month.
The combination of sticky oil prices and a fragile bond market

suggests that the SEK rebound may struggle to extend in the near
term. A return to 11.00 now hinges on some easing of Middle East
tensions and/or further ﬁscal risks weighing on EUR.
We expect the Riksbank to hike 25bp in November (now 18bp
priced in), and to maintain a hawkish bias. Policymakers likely
hope that will help SEK, but external factors remain too dominant.
We retain a bearish EUR/SEK proﬁle, which relies heavily on some
improvement in geopolitical conditions and, to some extent, in

French risk weighing on the euro for longer.

Our baseline view is that Norges Bank won’t hike rates again in

Inﬂation remains the key policy driver. September inﬂation was
softer than consensus, with the headline rate rising to 3.4% and

improvement in coming months to remain on hold.

rapid de-escalation in the Gulf and sharp oil price decline. Neither
appears imminent at this stage. EUR/NOK is incidentally being
dampened by the soft euro, which is oﬀsetting the poorer liquidity
environment.

We now think the Bank of Canada will hike rates in December.

The Kiwi dollar has been a big underperformer as of late. External

our NZD/USD lower but still see upside potential next year.

Document 313: Rules of Engagement_ Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties (2026-09-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rules of Engagement_ Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties (2026-09-28).pdf

Deutsche Bank Research Institute

### Page 2

Miha Hribernik | Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties |  September 2026
The Trump-Xi meeting in Washington met our key expectation: It delivered a subdued-yet-constructive summit resulting in a modest number of concrete deliverables that will minimise the near-term risks of abrupt
decoupling, a security crisis, or a disruptive trade war. This was the best plausible outcome for markets, given the structural nature of China-US competition. The brief two-month extension of the Busan agreement until

10 Jan 2027 will further delay new tariffs and export controls on both sides. It will also increase pressure to deliver a broader trade and investment package, and further trade-truce extensions, by the end of the year. We
anticipate more talks between US Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng in Q4 as both sides seek a larger deal.
For China, stability with Washington will likely remain a priority as it manages rising trade tensions with the EU and prepares for top leadership reshuffles at its 2027 Party Congress. Combined with shifting US public

perceptions of China, these factors could create near-term space for more dealmaking on the sidelines of the upcoming APEC (18-19 Nov) and G20 (14-15 Dec) summits. As our surveys below show, US voters are now
more open to increased trade and investment with China, even at the cost of geopolitical concessions, while concerns over the cost of living have intensified. Both trends are now at their highest levels since April.
Nevertheless, political guardrails are not a panacea for a relationship that remains characterized by mistrust and competing national security priorities. As the next slide will show, the outlook for China-US relations

becomes more complex beyond this near-term horizon….
Tactical détente: The near-term outlook
Political commitment and shifting domestic priorities create space for stability into 2027

### Page 3

Miha Hribernik | Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties |  September 2026
An analysis of the geopolitical calendar reveals a complex slate of tariffs, import curbs, export controls, and sensitive pol itical events. Even as leader-level diplomacy has reduced the risk of a near-term breakdown, it
has done little to stem the tide of new bilateral economic restrictions. These have proliferated during 2026 to include new e xport controls, sanctions, and tariffs (see list), with significant implications for high-growth

industries including drones, AI, electric vehicles, and robotics.
Competition is unlikely to slow, and events could repeatedly test the fragile trade truce. Q4 2026 will see further China-US dialogue and China-EU trade talks. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting increased
European trade pressure, Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. But the coming months will also bring challenges in the form of new US overcapacity tariffs and polysilicon levies, while next year will usher

in curbs on critical minerals and additional semiconductor tariffs. The run-up to the 10 Jan Busan truce expiry could also bring new economic restrictions as both sides seek negotiating leverage. Both countries will also
hold major political events (China’s Party Congress in 2027 and US elections in 2028), with potential implications for bilateral ties, while Taiwan’s Jan 2028 election will be another key watchpoint.
But these are just the events whose outcomes and impacts we can estimate in advance. Understanding probable China-US developments that are not on the calendar is equally important for investors. We should

assume that competitive dynamics across myriad policy issues will drive other developments and policy changes, often announce d with little or no advance notice. We examine some of the key issues in the next slide.
The policy horizon: Key events to watch (2026–2029)
The next several years will see more summits, tariffs, major political events, and key elections

### Page 4

Miha Hribernik | Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties |  September 2026
Unless consistently managed, structural competition could move markets and test stability through the end of the
decade and beyond. We highlight five watchpoints for markets:
1. The AI race: Dialogue is set to strengthen AI safety guardrails, but national and economic security imperatives
mean it is unlikely to significantly slow the China-US AI race, much less meaningfully reverse existing restrictions.

Any attempts to pace AI development would touch on questions of tech restrictions, including Beijing’s perceptions
of US technological containment, which could increase amid potential restrictions on remote access to AI chips and
Chinese data centre components.
2. Tariff pressures: Trade tensions have subsided given a lower US trade deficit and the new Board of Trade, which is
set to reduce tariffs on US$60 billion of non-sensitive goods. But significant differences persist: Washington plans

to unveil new polysilicon and semiconductor tariffs, as well as overcapacity levies on 16 economies (including
China). The US is also seeking to restrict Chinese inputs in North American supply chains and target alleged
transshipment, while Beijing shores up its supply chain dominance in areas of US import dependence, such as
advanced drone components and processed critical minerals – which continue to be a source of friction.
3. Regulatory toolkits: The wider tech sector remains central to expanding export controls and restrictions. China is

strengthening its toolkit. Its 2026 codification of anti-derisking regulations strengthens Beijing’s ability to take
action over foreign sanctions or export controls. Meanwhile, its outbound investment regulations, effective 1 Jul,
cover technology, data, talent and capital. In the US, Congress is drafting legislation to change outbound
investment reporting requirements for biotech and biopharma, restrict government procurement of humanoid
robots and solar equipment, and ban the use of China-linked connected vehicles on military installations.

4. Geopolitical stress-tests: The war in Iran is unlikely to derail relations in the near term, but the conflict has strained
bilateral ties. Any potential broadening of the conflict in scale and intensity, or a further expansion of US sanctions
that impact Chinese interests, could test guardrails. Additionally, while the probability of conflict in the Taiwan
Strait remains low, differences over Taiwan could remain unresolved across successive administrations in both

Washington and Beijing into the 2030s and beyond.
5. Political anchors: Two domestic events will shape relations into the 2030s. Beijing’s priority is stability ahead of the
late 2027 Party Congress. For China, dialogue can help reduce the risk of costly escalation, potentially slow the
pace of new US economic restrictions, and buy time for efforts to strengthen self-reliance in strategic sectors such
as semiconductors (where China is aiming for 80% self-sufficiency by 2030).

In the US, a durable bipartisan consensus on China could make visible concessions to Beijing increasingly difficult
ahead of the 2028 presidential election. Recent bipartisan criticism of the Trump administration’s decision to allow
limited Nvidia H200 chip sales to China reflects the political constraints facing a transactional approach to China,
raising the prospect that guardrails will strain closer to election day.
Structural strains: Testing the guardrails

Navigating the trade, technology, and regional pressure points

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.
In the spotlight for October 2026: US-Iran war and EU-China relations

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026

### Page 7

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,
Lebanon and Iran. This is the first Knesset since 1988 to actually complete a full four-year term without
early dissolution. Benjamim Netanyahu remains the dominant single figure but faces a fragmented field:
Naftali Bennett’s new “Together” party is polling as the main rival bloc, alongside Gadi Eisenkot’s Yashar,

Benny Gantz’s Blue and White, and the further right Ben Gvir and Smotrich factions.
Israel’s proportional system means the election is effectively about coalition formation. Key faultlines to
watch: the ultra-Orthodox draft-exemption fight, how the war’s legacy and hostage/reconstruction issues
in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026

### Page 8

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
The foreign ministers’ meeting comes ahead of the December Leaders’ Summit in Miami under the US’
chairmanship of the G20 this year. Trump’s G20 year has organized around four expert tracks – trade,
innovation, energy and deregulation. The session is framed around economic prosperity, energy security
and technological innovation, a much more transactional focus than previous foreign ministerials, which

typically focused on geopolitical crisis management.
It will be a test of whether the G20 can sustain cooperation amid intensifying geopolitical fragmentation.
In addition, Russia has confirmed its intent to actively participate in the meeting. This would further
normalize Russia’s multilateral engagement. For Europe and other partners, a key question will be the
extent to which the G20 can be a forum for broader international cooperation.

Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• Consumer spending is holding up, and we expect real PCE to rise at around a 3.0% annualized rate
in Q3.
Consumer spending has been mixed, yet resilient in recent months. After a soft July, driven largely by

weaker durable goods purchases, retail sales rebounded strongly in August, rising at a 1.2% monthly
clip amid broadly distributed gains across retailers. The latest data have led us to lift our near-term
growth forecast, and we now look for real PCE to rise at a 3.2% annualized clip in Q3. Previous data
will also likely be revised higher on firmer control group sales data and strong services revenue in Q2,
suggesting recent consumer momentum remains intact. All told, the consumer is still in decent shape

and has capacity to keep spending. Real income growth remains modest but is beginning to improve
as inflation eases and the labor market remains broadly supportive suggesting continued consumption
even if that pace of spending may moderate into year-end.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build-out is still king in terms of the contribution to business investment, but the
broadening in capex spending beyond the tech sector is taking root.

AI-related investment continues to dominate capital allocation. There are no signs of firms scaling
back their outlays now, though at some point as AI investment matures, merely sustaining spending
at elevated levels will provide a smaller incremental boost to growth than it has in recent quarters.
Beyond AI, the tentative signs we've identified that signal a broadening in the investment backdrop
are getting a bit louder. The ISM manufacturing index has been in expansion territory for eight-

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
straight months, and durable goods orders have firmed across a wider range of industries. Part of
this improvement likely reflects delayed capital spending plans finally moving forward, but inventory
rebuilding after five consecutive quarters of drawdown may be lending some support as well.

Investment: Residential
• Residential construction looks likely to taper through the rest of the year while mortgage rates and
new home inventories remain elevated.
Headwinds have intensified against residential construction. The trend in single-family construction is
downshifting, emblematic of the affordability challenges depressing buyer demand. As of this writing,

mortgage rates have risen to over 7.0% and builder confidence has declined alongside. Sales stuck in
a low gear mean that new home listings are inching higher, further discouraging new development.
Multifamily construction appears to be holding up but faces limited potential upside with vacancy
rates elevated across much of the South. We still see a potential for marginal improvement in 2027,
but higher mortgage rates dampen our outlook.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures

• Construction appears to be gaining steam, led by data centers and power. However, sluggish
commercial starts elsewhere and high interest rates are likely to keep growth relatively modest.
The AI infrastructure build out is supporting modest growth in nonresidential construction. After
moderating for a full year, private nonresidential outlays rose from April to July. Data centers and
power are propelling current momentum. Though construction has also strengthened across sports

facilities and communication infrastructure, commercial projects remain stuck in low gear. Categories
like warehousing and retail remain soft, and overall architectural billings have improved only modestly.
Looking ahead, a recent breakout in megaprojects is likely to support additional data center and
manufacturing development, while higher interest rates limit overall growth.
